\"\"\"Assert the subprocess exit code (COMP-010.4).\n\n        Default behaviour asserts ``equals=0``. ``equals`` and ``in_set``\n        are mutually exclusive — supplying both raises ``ValueError`` {"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01LuMHabjaueUfuvVfAdhdQa","type":"tool_result","content":"1\t\"\"\"COMP-014 install_hooks adapter for per-eval HOME (Task T02.14 / D-0034).\n2\t\n3\tThis module is the single integration point between the cliEval harness's\n4\tper-eval ``HOME`` (materialized by :class:`HomeIsolation` in T02.11) and\n5\tthe production hook installer at :mod:`superclaude.cli.install_hooks`.\n6\tThe adapter has two jobs:\n7\t\n8\t1. **Reuse, do not duplicate.** Delegate the script-copy + settings.json\n9\t   merge to the existing ``install_hooks`` so the per-eval hook surface\n10\t   is exactly the production user-install surface. A separate copy\n11\t   pipeline would drift; the COMP-014 reuse contract pins this to the\n12\t   one installer.\n13\t2. **Deploy ``hooks.json`` verbatim.** ``install_hooks`` merges hook\n14\t   registrations *into* ``settings.json`` but never copies the source\n15\t   ``hooks.json`` to the target HOME. The acceptance criteria for T02.14\n16\t   require ``<home_path>/.claude/hooks.json`` to be byte-identical to\n17\t   ``src/superclaude/hooks/hooks.json`` so post-mortem inspection and the\n18\t   FR-G* gate tests can SHA256-compare the source-of-truth registrations\n19\t   against what the per-eval HOME shipped. The adapter performs this\n20\t   second copy itself.\n21\t\n22\tDefense-in-depth: a pre-flight refusal asserts ``home_path`` is not the\n23\treal ``~/.claude/`` (resolves both sides; rejects any equality or\n24\tdescendant relationship). This complements but does not replace the\n25\t:class:`HomeIsolation` containment guard — the adapter MAY be invoked\n26\tdirectly by tests, so the guard must live here too.\n27\t\n28\tIdempotency: re-invoking ``deploy_hooks_to`` against the same\n29\t``home_path`` produces identical filesystem state. ``install_hooks``\n30\titself is idempotent (existing scripts are skipped without ``--force``;\n31\tthe settings.json merge skips matcher collisions). The verbatim\n32\t``hooks.json`` copy uses ``shutil.copy2`` which overwrites with the same\n33\tbytes when the source is unchanged.\n34\t\n35\tError contract: any failure inside the adapter raises\n36\t:class:`HookDeployFailed` with an :attr:`HookDeployFailed.error_tag`\n37\tattribute the EvalRunner (T03.x) propagates into\n38\t``EvalRunner.outcome.artifacts``. The tag is a short kebab-case\n39\tidentifier the orchestrator buckets ERRORED evals by without parsing\n40\thuman-readable strings.\n41\t\n42\tCross-links\n43\t===========\n44\t\n45\t* COMP-006 :class:`HomeIsolation` (T02.11 / D-0032) — supplies the\n46\t  ``home_path`` argument.\n47\t* NFR-ISO2 atomic-setup wrapper (T02.13 / D-0033) — invokes the\n48\t  adapter from inside ``HomeIsolation.setup``'s post-mkdtemp block in\n49\t  a future wiring task.\n50\t* :func:`superclaude.cli.install_hooks.install_hooks` — the underlying\n51\t  installer this adapter wraps.\n52\t\"\"\"\n53\t\n54\tfrom __future__ import annotations\n55\t\n56\timport shutil\n57\tfrom pathlib import Path\n58\t\n59\tfrom superclaude.cli.install_hooks import install_hooks\n60\t\n61\t__all__ = [\n62\t    \"HookDeployFailed\",\n63\t    \"deploy_hooks_to\",\n64\t]\n65\t\n66\t\n67\t# Relative paths under the per-eval HOME that the adapter writes to.\n68\t_SETTINGS_RELPATH = (\".claude\", \"settings.json\")\n69\t_HOOKS_JSON_RELPATH = (\".claude\", \"hooks.json\")\n70\t\n71\t\n72\tclass HookDeployFailed(Exception):\n73\t    \"\"\"Raised by :func:`deploy_hooks_to` when hook deployment cannot complete.\n74\t\n75\t    Carries a short kebab-case :attr:`error_tag` the orchestrator\n76\t    (:class:`EvalRunner`, T03.x) routes into\n77\t    ``EvalRunner.outcome.artifacts`` so failed evals can be bucketed\n78\t    without parsing the human-readable message.\n79\t\n80\t    Attributes:\n81\t        error_tag: Short identifier for the failure mode. One of:\n82\t\n83\t            * ``\"refused-real-home\"`` — ``home_path`` resolved to (or\n84\t              under) the real ``~/.claude/``; defense-in-depth guard\n85\t              fired BEFORE any filesystem write.\n86\t            * ``\"install-hooks-failed\"`` — the underlying\n87\t              :func:`install_hooks` returned ``success=False``. The\n88\t              installer's message is preserved in :attr:`detail`.\n89\t            * ``\"hooks-json-copy-failed\"`` — the verbatim\n90\t              ``hooks.json`` copy raised :class:`OSError`. Call :meth:`spawn` to actually launch the child. ``spawn``\n       returns the underlying :class:`ClaudeProcess` so the caller can\n       drive ``.wait()`` / ``.terminate()`` directly — the adapter does\n       not interpose on those lifecycle methods, keeping the\n       :class:`ClaudeProcess` contract unaltered.\n\n    Parameters\n    ----------\n    home:\n        :class:`HomeIsolation` instance whose ``setup`` has already\n        materialized the per-eval HOME. The adapter calls\n        :meth:`HomeIsolation.env` at spawn time; calling ``env``\n        without ``setup`` would itself raise :class:`RuntimeError`,\n        which the adapter does not catch.\n    prompt:\n        Prompt text delivered to ``claude --print`` over stdin (see\n        :meth:`ClaudeProcess.start`).\n    output_file:\n        Path where ``claude``'s stdout will be written.\n    error_file:\n        Path where ``claude``'s stderr will be written. Distinct file\n        handle from ``output_file`` — required by the AC.\n    cwd:\n        Working directory the child should inherit. Must exist on disk\n        (the adapter resolves it eagerly and raises\n        :class:`ClaudeProcessAdapterError` otherwise). Defaults to the\n        per-eval HOME directory when not supplied.\n    extra_env:\n        Optional env-var overlay applied BEFORE\n        :meth:`HomeIsolation.env`. Lets callers inject capability\n        toggles (e.g. ``CLAUDE_DISABLE_MCP``) without overriding the\n        isolation keys; the merge order is ``os.environ → extra_env →\n        HomeIsolation.env`` so the isolation layer always wins.\n    timeout_seconds, max_turns, model, permission_flag, output_format,\n    extra_args, on_spawn, on_signal, on_exit, tool_write_mode:\n        Forwarded verbatim to :class:`ClaudeProcess`. See its\n        docstring for the contract.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    def __init__(\n        self,\n        *,\n        home: \"HomeIsolation\",\n        prompt: str,\n        output_file: Path,\n        error_file: Path,\n        cwd: Path | None = None,\n        extra_env: dict[str, str] | None = None,\n        timeout_seconds: int = 6300,\n        max_turns: int = 100,\n        model: str = \"\",\n        permission_flag: str = \"--dangerously-skip-permissions\",\n        output_format: str = \"stream-json\",\n        extra_args: list[str] | None = None,\n        on_spawn: Callable[[int], None] | None = None,\n        on_signal: Callable[[int, str], None] | None = None,\n        on_exit: Callable[[int, int | None], None] | None = None,\n        tool_write_mode: bool = False,\n    ) -> None:\n        if output_file == error_file:\n            raise ClaudeProcessAdapterError(\n                \"output_file and error_file must be distinct paths \"\n                \"(AC: stdout/stderr separation)\"\n            )\n\n        self._home = home\n        self._prompt = prompt\n        self._output_file = Path(output_file)\n        self._error_file = Path(error_file)\n        self._extra_env: dict[str, str] = dict(extra_env) if extra_env else {}\n        self._timeout_seconds = timeout_seconds\n        self._max_turns = max_turns\n        self._model = model\n        self._permission_flag = permission_flag\n        self._output_format = output_format\n        self._extra_args: list[str] = list(extra_args) if extra_args else []\n        self._on_spawn = on_spawn\n        self._on_signal = on_signal\n        self._on_exit = on_exit\n        self._tool_write_mode = tool_write_mode\n\n        # Resolve cwd eagerly so spawn-time errors are localized to\n        # construction (the orchestrator pre-builds adapters and only\n        # spawns later — fail-fast keeps the failure surface tight).\n        self._cwd: Path = Path(cwd) if cwd is not None else None  # type: ignore[assignment]\n        # Defer existence check until we know whether to default to\n        # home_path. HomeIsolation.home_path raises before setup, which\n        # is the failure mode we want here too.\n        if self._cwd is None:\n            self._cwd = self._home.home_path\n        if not self._cwd.exists():\n            raise ClaudeProcessAdapterError(\n                f\"cwd {self._cwd!s} does not exist on disk\"\n            )\n\n    # ------------------------------------------------------------------\n    # Env assembly\n    # ------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n    def build_env(self) -> dict[str, str]:\n        \"\"\"Return the env dict the spawned ``claude`` will see.\n\n        Merge order (later keys win):\n\n        1. ``os.environ`` snapshot (taken inside :meth:`ClaudeProcess.\n           build_env`, after stripping ``CLAUDECODE`` /\n           ``CLAUDE_CODE_ENTRYPOINT`` to prevent nested-session\n           detection).\n        2. ``extra_env`` (capability toggles, debug flags).\n        3. :meth:`HomeIsolation.env` — ``HOME``, ``CLAUDE_SESSION_ID``,\n           and optional ``CLAUDE_FAKE_TIME_OFFSET``. Isolation always\n           wins so a misconfigured ``extra_env`` cannot leak the\n           operator's real HOME into the child.\n\n        The returned dict is the same object semantics as\n        :meth:`ClaudeProcess.build_env` (i.e., a new ``dict[str, str]``\n        the caller owns).\n        \"\"\"\n\n        # Build a throwaway ClaudeProcess just for its build_env helper.\n        # The helper does the CLAUDECODE / CLAUDE_CODE_ENTRYPOINT\n        # scrubbing that the adapter must inherit verbatim.\n        merged: dict[str, str] = {}\n        merged.update(self._extra_env)\n        merged.update(self._home.env())\n        proc = self._build_process()\n        return proc.build_env(env_vars=merged)\n\n    def build_command(self) -> list[str]:\n        \"\"\"Return the argv that will be ``exec``-ed by :class:`ClaudeProcess`.\n\n        Useful for tests and dry-run reporters; the argv is identical\n        to what :meth:`ClaudeProcess.build_command` would emit because\n        the adapter delegates directly. No anthropic SDK calls are\n        involved in either branch.\n        \"\"\"\n\n        return self._build_process().build_command()\n\n    # ------------------------------------------------------------------\n    # Process construction\n    # ------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n    def _build_process(self) -> ClaudeProcess:\n        \"\"\"Construct a fresh (not started) :class:`ClaudeProcess`.\n\n        The env merge happens via the ``env_vars`` kwarg so\n        ``ClaudeProcess`` re-applies its own ``os.environ.copy`` +\n        scrub on every call (consistent with its upstream contract).\n        \"\"\"\n\n        merged_env: dict[str, str] = {}\n        merged_env.update(self._extra_env)\n        # HomeIsolation.env wins by construction (applied last).\n        merged_env.update(self._home.env())\n        return ClaudeProcess(\n            prompt=self._prompt,\n            output_file=self._output_file,\n            error_file=self._error_file,\n            max_turns=self._max_turns,\n            model=self._model,\n            permission_flag=self._permission_flag,\n            timeout_seconds=self._timeout_seconds,\n            output_format=self._output_format,\n            extra_args=self._extra_args,\n            on_spawn=self._on_spawn,\n            on_signal=self._on_signal,\n            on_exit=self._on_exit,\n            env_vars=merged_env,\n            tool_write_mode=self._tool_write_mode,\n        )\n\n    # ------------------------------------------------------------------\n    # Spawn\n    # ------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n    @property\n    def cwd(self) -> Path:\n        \"\"\"Working directory the child will inherit at fork time.\"\"\"\n\n        return self._cwd\n\n    @property\n    def home(self) -> \"HomeIsolation\":\n        \"\"\"The :class:`HomeIsolation` driving env injection.\"\"\"\n\n        return self._home\n\n    def spawn(self) -> ClaudeProcess:","numLines":200,"startLine":120,"totalLines":370}}}

Purpose\n13\t\n14\tOQ-2 (roadmap.md:110) asks: *\"Concrete content of E3–E15 manifest entries.\"*\n15\tThe design-spec (§5) shows only E1 and E2 parameterize as exemplars; E3–E15 are deferred.\n16\t\n17\tThis spec freezes the **inputs, expects, and capability tags** for each of the 13 remaining bodies so that T05.07 … T05.21 may author them without further design churn.\n18\t\n19\t## 2. Design constraints\n20\t\n21\t1. **Coverage:** every hook event type in `src/superclaude/hooks/hooks.json` MUST be exercised by ≥1 eval among {E1, E2.1–3, E3 … E15}.\n22\t2. **Determinism:** every body MUST pass deterministically on a clean per-eval HOME (FR-ISO2) — no time-of-day, no network, no shared mutable state. (3-run determinism is the per-task AC.)\n23\t3. **No OQ-8 dependency:** E3–E15 MUST NOT require `CLAUDE_FAKE_TIME_OFFSET`. The original design-spec note tying E3 to \"30-min freshness tests\" is **superseded**; freshness-staleness via time offset is deferred until OQ-8 closes (separate follow-up).\n24\t4. **Expect.* primitive only:** every body's assertions MUST be expressible via the v1 DSL (`file.exists`, `file.absent`, `jsonl.contains_event`, `jsonl.event_count`, `settings_json.has_registration`, `exit_code.equals`, `stderr.contains`, `stdout.contains`, `duration.less_than`, `duration.greater_than`) plus the YAML `callback:` escape (D-4) for E14.\n25\t5. **Capability gating:** evals requiring a live MCP server soft-skip under `--no-mcp` with `skip_reason` populated (FR-CAP1).\n26\t\n27\t## 3. Design constraints\n\n1. **Coverage:** every hook event type in `src/superclaude/hooks/hooks.json` MUST be exercised by ≥1 eval among {E1, E2.1–3, E3 … E15}.\n2. **Determinism:** every body MUST pass deterministically on a clean per-eval HOME (FR-ISO2) — no time-of-day, no network, no shared mutable state. The original design-spec note tying E3 to \"30-min freshness tests\" is **superseded**; freshness-staleness via time offset is deferred until OQ-8 closes (separate follow-up).\n4. **Expect.* primitive only:** every body's assertions MUST be expressible via the v1 DSL (`file.exists`, `file.absent`, `jsonl.contains_event`, `jsonl.event_count`, `settings_json.has_registration`, `exit_code.equals`, `stderr.contains`, `stdout.contains`, `duration.less_than`, `duration.greater_than`) plus the YAML `callback:` escape (D-4) for E14.\n5. **Capability gating:** evals requiring a live MCP server soft-skip under `--no-mcp` with `skip_reason` populated (FR-CAP1).\n\n## 3. :meth:`Expect.stdout` /\n    :meth:`Expect.stderr` still get clean predicate matches.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    return ANSI_ESCAPE_RE.sub(\"\", text)\n\n\ndef _make_failure(\n    ctx: EvalContext,\n    *,\n    name: str,\n    expected: Any,\n    actual: Any,\n    message: str,\n    artifact_ref: Optional[str] = None,\n    exc: Optional[BaseException] = None,\n) -> ExpectFailure:\n    \"\"\"Build an :class:`ExpectFailure` record from primitive-local state.\"\"\"\n\n    tb = \"\".join(traceback.format_exception(exc)) if exc is not None else None\n    return ExpectFailure(\n        eval_id=ctx.eval_spec.id,\n        expect_id=name,\n        expect_name=name,\n        expected=expected,\n        actual=actual,\n        message=message,\n        artifact_ref=artifact_ref,\n        traceback=tb,\n    )\n\n\ndef _unified_diff(expected: str, actual: str, *, name: str) -> str:\n    \"\"\"Return a unified diff snippet between ``expected`` and ``actual``.\"\"\"\n\n    diff_lines = JSONL log records it.\"\"\"\n\n    fn.__name__ = name\n    return fn\n\n\ndef _timed_result(\n    name: str,\n    builder: Callable[[], tuple[bool, str, Mapping[str, Any], duration_sec=duration,\n        failure=failure,\n    )\n\n\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n# Public DSL surface\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n\nclass Expect:\n    \"\"\"COMP-010 / FR-EXP1 assertion primitive namespace.\n\n    The class itself is never compiled = re.compile(regex) if regex is not None else None\n\n        def _run(ctx: EvalContext) -> ExpectResult:\n            def _build() -> tuple[bool, str, Mapping[str, Any], bool(exists):\n                    msg = f\"file existence: expected {bool(exists)} got {file_exists}\"\n                    return False, msg, {\"path\": str(resolved), \"exists\": file_exists}, \"\"\"Assert the subprocess exit code (COMP-010.4).\n\n        Default behaviour asserts ``equals=0``. ``equals`` and ``in_set``\n        are mutually exclusive — supplying both raises ``ValueError`` Its observable side-effects in the\nper-eval HOME are:\n\n```\n$HOME/.claude/state/auggie-first-pending/<session_id>.txt   (input)\n$HOME/.claude/logs/auggie-first.jsonl                       (output)\n```\n\nThe hook's load-bearing branch (script line 25) is:\n\n```bash\nSTICKY=\"$STATE_DIR/auggie-first-pending/$SESSION_ID.txt\"\nif [ -f \"$STICKY\" ]; then\n    rm -f \"$STICKY\" 2>/dev/null || true\n    ... printf '{\"ts\":..., \"session_id\":..., \"event\":\"sticky_cleared\", \"tool\":...}' >> \"$AUGGIE_LOG\" ...\nfi\n```\n\n**Implication:** the hook only emits `sticky_cleared` IF the sticky\npre-existed. Therefore asserting the JSONL event is a **sufficient\nproxy** for the full \"set → clear\" lifecycle:\n\n- `sticky_cleared` present ⇒ sticky was present before the tool call\n  (set) AND the tool call matched the hook's matcher (clear).\n- `sticky_cleared` absent ⇒ either no matching tool call OR no sticky\n  existed pre-call — either way the lifecycle did NOT complete.\n\nThis proxy is what makes the body shape expressible under the current\nExpect.* primitive surface (D-4 / T04.0x) **without** session-id\ntemplate substitution (see §4).\n\n## 3. Frozen body shape\n\nThe body shape lands in `suites/real.yaml` under the E1 entry that\nalready carries the FR-CAP1 metadata. New body additions:\n\n| Field | Value |\n|---|---|\n| **inputs[0].prompt** | `\"Use mcp__auggie__codebase-retrieval to summarise the auggie-flag-clear hook in src/superclaude/hooks/scripts/.\"` |\n| **inputs[0].expect_tool_call** | `mcp__auggie__codebase-retrieval` |\n| **expects[0]** | `file: { path: logs/auggie-first.jsonl, exists: true, contains: '\"event\":\"sticky_cleared\"' }` |\n| **expects[1]** | `exit_code: { equals: 0 }` |\n\nThe `expect_tool_call` value lights up the FR-COV1 coverage gate (per\n`coverage.py:_iter_eval_tool_calls`), so the PostToolUse\n`mcp__auggie__*` matcher receives the eval-credit needed for E1 to\nregister as \"covered\" alongside E2.1.\n\n**Rationale for `contains` substring (not `jsonl.assert_any`):** the\nv1 Expect.jsonl primitive's `assert_each` / `assert_any` arguments are\nPython callables (`expect.py:269-283`) and have no YAML representation.\nExpressing the event-presence assertion via `Expect.file(contains=...)`\nagainst the JSONL byte-stream is the YAML-friendly equivalent for v1\nand matches D-0082 §2 constraint 4 (\"Expect.* primitive only\").\n\n## 4. Documented gap — literal `file_absent(state/.../<sid>.txt)`\n\nThe original design-spec §5.1 sketch for E1 included a literal\n`file.exists(path=\"state/auggie-first-pending/{session_id}.txt\")` /\n`file.absent(...)` pre/post pair. That assertion is **deferred** at\nT05.02 for two reasons:\n\n1. **No template substitution layer:** `session_id` is allocated by the\n   orchestrator at runtime (HomeIsolation.session_id, stamped into\n   `CLAUDE_SESSION_ID`) and is not known at YAML-author time. The\n   current YAML manifest has no `{session_id}` template-resolution\n   pass.\n2. **No pre/post snapshot mechanism:** Expect.* primitives all execute\n   in the **post-execution** phase (per `runner.py` 7-step lifecycle).\n   There is no \"pre-tool-call snapshot\" primitive that would let the\n   YAML assert \"before the matched tool call, the sticky file existed\".\n\nBoth gaps are tracked as follow-ups (not blocking T05.02):\n\n- **FU-T05.02-A** — add `{session_id}` placeholder substitution in\n  `loader.py` so manifests can reference `home_path /\n  state/auggie-first-pending/{session_id}.txt` literally.\n- **FU-T05.02-B** — extend Expect.* with a pre-hook snapshot primitive\n  (or an EvalContext field carrying the pre-spawn FS image) so\n  pre/post differential assertions are expressible.\n\nUntil those land, the §3 body shape (sticky_cleared event + exit 0) is\nthe verifiable contract; the literal file_absent assertion is a\ndocumentation-only contract.\n\n## 5. `--no-mcp` soft-skip behavior\n\nThe existing suite-level wiring carries E1's capability gate:\n\n```yaml\n# real.yaml (unchanged)\noptional_capabilities:\n  - { name: mcp_server.auggie, gate_flag: \"--no-mcp\", failure_mode: skip }\n\nevals:\n  - id: E1\n    requires: [mcp_server.auggie]\n    ...\n```\n\nWhen the runner is invoked with `--no-mcp`, the FR-CAP1 path in\n`commands.py` adds `mcp_server.auggie` to the disabled-capabilities\nset; CapabilityGates therefore returns SKIPPED with\n`skip_reason=\"--no-mcp\"` / `skip_flag_triggered=\"--no-mcp\"` before any\nHOME setup or PTY spawn. This matches D-0082 §2 constraint 5.\n\n**Net behaviour matrix:**\n\n| Invocation | E1 outcome | Why |\n|---|---|---|\n| `eval run --suite real` (no flags) | RUNS | capability available, body executes |\n| `eval run --suite real --no-mcp` | SKIPPED (skip_reason=`--no-mcp`) | FR-CAP1 soft-skip via `optional_capabilities` |\n| `eval run --suite real --no-pty` | SKIPPED (skip_reason=`--no-pty`) | per-eval `no_pty: skip` tag (R-077 / D-0077) |\n| `eval run --suite real --eval E1 --no-mcp --no-pty` | SKIPPED | `--no-pty` short-circuits first per `commands.py` |\n\n## 6. Verification\n\nPer phase-5-tasklist.md T05.02:\n\n```bash\nuv run superclaude eval run --suite real --eval E1\n```\n\n**Today's runner state:** the M-1 runner does not yet drive PTY-based\nprompt injection from `inputs[0].prompt` — that wiring lands later in\nPhase 5 (per the design-spec §6 PTY harness component). T05.02\nauthors the manifest body; verification is therefore performed via:\n\n- (a) `eval describe --suite real --eval E1` rendering the new\n  inputs/expects rows; and\n- (b) `eval run --suite real --eval E1 --no-mcp` resolving to\n  SKIPPED with `skip_reason=\"--no-mcp\"` (proves capability gate wiring\n  intact and body is loadable).\n\nFull end-to-end PTY execution proof is rolled into the relevant\nrunner-completion task (downstream of T05.02).\n\nSee `evidence.md` for the verification command transcripts.\n\n## 7. But neither half is\ndirectly expressible today:\n\n1. **Pre-call snapshot** — the runner only invokes `expects[]` after\n   the subprocess has exited (`runner.py` step 6 of 7). There is no\n   \"freeze FS at t=spawn\" primitive.\n2. **Post-call file path** — the path contains the orchestrator-allocated\n   `session_id`, which is not known at YAML-author time. The loader\n   does not template-substitute `{session_id}` against\n   `EvalContext.home_isolation.session_id`.\n\nThe `auggie-flag-clear.sh` hook, by its `if [ -f \"$STICKY\" ]` guard,\ncollapses the two-step assertion into one **observable outcome**: the\n`sticky_cleared` JSONL line is emitted IFF the lifecycle ran. Because\nthe JSONL path (`logs/auggie-first.jsonl`) is fixed (not\nsession-templated), `Expect.file(contains='\"event\":\"sticky_cleared\"')`\ncovers the entire contract under the current primitive surface.\n\n## Why `Expect.file` and not `Expect.jsonl` for the event predicate\n\n`Expect.jsonl` requires `assert_each` or `assert_any` predicates as\nPython callables (`expect.py:275-276`). These have no YAML wire form\nand would need either a `callback:` escape hatch (D-4) or a v2\ndeclarative DSL extension. Pushing E1 onto either of those would\neither delay the eval until a v2 DSL ships or bloat the suite with a\ncallback module for a tautological substring check.\n\nThe substring `'\"event\":\"sticky_cleared\"'` is uniquely identifying\nwithin `logs/auggie-first.jsonl` (the hook script's only event type\nin v1) and the JSONL byte-stream is a clean text file, so\n`Expect.file` is the v1-correct primitive choice. If the hook is\nextended in a later release to emit additional event types that\nincidentally contain the substring `sticky_cleared`, this would need\na tightening pass — flagged as risk-low / followup-only.\n\n## Why a concrete prompt string and not a placeholder\n\n`inputs[0].prompt` is presented as a real English instruction\n(\"Use mcp__auggie__codebase-retrieval to summarise the\nauggie-flag-clear hook in src/superclaude/hooks/scripts/.\") rather\nthan a placeholder, because:\n\n- The PTY-driver wiring will eventually feed this string verbatim to\n  the Claude subprocess.\n- The chosen instruction biases the subprocess toward calling the\n  named MCP tool, which is what the eval exercises.\n- It documents intent to a human reader of the manifest.\n\nThe exact wording is non-load-bearing: any prompt that biases the\nsubprocess to call `mcp__auggie__codebase-retrieval` exactly once\nwill satisfy the eval. The `expect_tool_call` field is what the\ncoverage gate consumes.\n\n## Why E1 keeps `requires: [mcp_server.auggie]` despite `no_pty: skip`\n\nThe two flags gate independent dimensions:\n\n- `requires` + `optional_capabilities` → FR-CAP1 (MCP server present?)\n- `no_pty: skip` → R-077 (PTY harness usable?)\n\nA run with `--no-mcp` but not `--no-pty` still skips E1 via the\ncapability gate (correct). A run with `--no-pty` but not `--no-mcp`\nskips E1 via the PTY gate (also correct — every body in the \"real\"\nsuite drives a PTY). Both gates can fire independently; FR-CAP1 must\nnot be removed just because `no_pty: skip` happens to cover the\ncommon case.\n\n## Determinism\n\nThe body is deterministic in the \"passes/fails the same way every\nrun on a clean per-eval HOME\" sense:\n\n- the auggie tool call is the same every run (prompt is static);\n- the hook's `sticky_cleared` event is the same every run when the\n  sticky pre-existed;\n- the JSONL byte-stream is fresh per-eval (HomeIsolation, FR-ISO2);\n- the timestamp on the JSONL line varies but is not asserted against.\n\n3-run determinism is the per-task acceptance criterion (D-0082 §2.2).\n"},"caller":{"type":"direct"}}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"stop_details":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":1,"cache_creation_input_tokens":3680,"cache_read_input_tokens":98653,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":3680,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0},"output_tokens":46,"service_tier":"standard","inference_geo":"not_available","speed":"standard"},"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"6ae5d5a8-fd07-4b5c-9877-f20a7e7751eb","uuid":"c0a64831-8c49-44b5-94ab-80bd10d3be0c"}

If the hook is\nextended in a later release to emit additional event types that\nincidentally contain the substring `sticky_cleared`, this would need\na tightening pass — flagged as risk-low / followup-only.\n\n## Why a concrete prompt string and not a placeholder\n\n`inputs[0].prompt` is presented as a real English instruction\n(\"Use mcp__auggie__codebase-retrieval to summarise the\nauggie-flag-clear hook in src/superclaude/hooks/scripts/.\") rather\nthan a placeholder, because:\n\n- The PTY-driver wiring will eventually feed this string verbatim to\n  the Claude subprocess.\n- The chosen instruction biases the subprocess toward calling the\n  named MCP tool, which is what the eval exercises.\n- It documents intent to a human reader of the manifest.\n\nThe exact wording is non-load-bearing: any prompt that biases the\nsubprocess to call `mcp__auggie__codebase-retrieval` exactly once\nwill satisfy the eval. The `expect_tool_call` field is what the\ncoverage gate consumes.\n\n## Why E1 keeps `requires: [mcp_server.auggie]` despite `no_pty: skip`\n\nThe two flags gate independent dimensions:\n\n- `requires` + `optional_capabilities` → FR-CAP1 (MCP server present?)\n- `no_pty: skip` → R-077 (PTY harness usable?)\n\nA run with `--no-mcp` but not `--no-pty` still skips E1 via the\ncapability gate (correct). A run with `--no-pty` but not `--no-mcp`\nskips E1 via the PTY gate (also correct — every body in the \"real\"\nsuite drives a PTY). Both gates can fire independently; FR-CAP1 must\nnot be removed just because `no_pty: skip` happens to cover the\ncommon case.\n\n## Determinism\n\nThe body is deterministic in the \"passes/fails the same way every\nrun on a clean per-eval HOME\" sense:\n\n- the auggie tool call is the same every run (prompt is static);\n- the hook's `sticky_cleared` event is the same every run when the\n  sticky pre-existed;\n- the JSONL byte-stream is fresh per-eval (HomeIsolation, FR-ISO2);\n- the timestamp on the JSONL line varies but is not asserted against.\n\n3-run determinism is the per-task acceptance criterion (D-0082 §2.2).\n","structuredPatch":[],"originalFile":null,"userModified":false}}
